```yaml
activation: if AI_Act in applicable_regs
phase_1A_role: 5_of_5
verified_relationship: COMPLEMENTARY (CRDA-deep, D-09.4: GDPR↔AI_Act = COMPLEMENTARY (SAME — Conditional-Y when provider/deployer is controller processing personal data via high-risk AI; GDPR RoPA + AI Act Annex IV + AI Act Art. 17 QMS stack on three parallel artefacts; AI Act Recital 78 explicit GDPR preservation; **no OJ-level consolidation rule for documentation**); NIS2↔AI_Act = COMPLEMENTARY RECOMMENDED-by-CRDA (Conditional-Y when NSIE deploys high-risk AI); CRA↔AI_Act = COMPLEMENTARY (SAME — Y when CRA-regulated product is also a high-risk AI system; CRA Annex VII + AI Act Annex IV + AI Act Art. 17 QMS stack on three parallel artefacts; **no OJ-level consolidation rule for documentation at D-09.4** — unlike the CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 risk-assessment consolidation rule at D-09.2, that rule covers the *risk-assessment object* not the *documentation object*); DORA↔AI_Act = COMPLEMENTARY RECOMMENDED-by-CRDA (Conditional-Y when DORA financial entity deploys high-risk AI))
```

**Objective.** Providers of high-risk AI systems **draw up and keep up-to-date the technical documentation of the high-risk AI system in accordance with Annex IV before that system is placed on the market or put into service** (Art. 11(1) AI Act), **keep the logs automatically recorded by the high-risk AI system to the extent under their control** (Art. 19(1) AI Act), and document the **quality management system** in written policies, procedures, and instructions covering at least the 13 enumerated aspects (a)-(m) (Art. 17(1) AI Act).

**CSF anchors (from `SR-AIACT-008` + `SR-AIACT-009` + `SR-AIACT-010` + `SR-AIACT-017` v0.3 security_rationale):**
ID.AM-08 (primary), PR.DS-12, GV.PO-01.
*(ID.AM-08 — Systems, hardware, software, services, and data are managed throughout their life cycle — anchors the AI Act Art. 11 + Art. 19(1) `logs referred to in Article 12(1)` + Art. 17(1) QMS documentation of 13 enumerated aspects mapped to NIST CSF 2.0 ID.AM-08 per CRDA DomainAnalysis §D-09.4 line 113 cross-validation; PR.DS-12 — Data managed consistent with risk strategy — anchors the AI Act Art. 17(1)(d) `data quality` + Art. 17(1)(g) `risk-management-system documentation cross-reference to Art. 9` per CRDA DomainAnalysis §D-09.4 line 113; GV.PO-01 — Organizational cybersecurity policy established, communicated, enforced — anchors the AI Act Art. 17(1) QMS `systematic and orderly manner in the form of written policies, procedures and instructions` mapped to NIST CSF 2.0 GV.PO-01 (cross to D-09.1 AI Act sub-SO). The three subcategories together enable the provider to demonstrate ex post, against the MSA + AI Office supervisory baseline, that the Annex IV technical documentation (Art. 11) + Art. 12(2) traceability + Art. 17(1) 13-element QMS documentation + Art. 19(1) provider logs were established before placing on the market and kept up-to-date during the AI lifecycle. **IMPORTANT — AI Act Recital 78 GDPR preservation**: AI Act Recital 78 explicitly preserves GDPR for personal-data processing — `protection of natural persons in relation to the processing of personal data … is governed by Regulation (EU) 2016/679`; providers that are also controllers maintain **parallel artefacts** (GDPR RoPA + AI Act Annex IV + AI Act Art. 17 QMS). **IMPORTANT — CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 risk-assessment consolidation does NOT extend to documentation**: the CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 unification rule at D-09.2 covers the *risk-assessment object*; there is no equivalent documentation-level consolidation rule at D-09.4; the two regimes maintain **parallel artefacts** (CRA Annex VII + AI Act Annex IV + AI Act Art. 17 QMS). The **T5 mapping error on AIA-C11 (Art. 12(4) does not exist in the OJ) is surfaced in SR-AIACT-009 `ambiguity_notes` without silent reinterpretation** — the AI Act Art. 12(4) reference is a mapping-error trap; the OJ only has Art. 12(1)/(2)/(3) (per SR-AIACT-009 ambiguity_notes).)*

#### Emergent tensions (from CRDA-deep §D-09.4)

**0 conditional emergent tensions** on the documentation duty itself — no pair was verified as CONTRADICTORY or CORRECTED. The 10 pairs are all SAME — 2 SAME — DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE + 3 SAME — COMPLEMENTARY-classified-by-CRDA + 1 SAME — DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE-recommended-by-CRDA + 4 SAME — COMPLEMENTARY-recommended-by-CRDA. **No CORRECTED, no CONTRADICTORY** pairs. **No scope-disjoint CONTRADICTORY** — the 3 `N (typically)` pairs (GDPR ↔ NIS 2 + GDPR ↔ CRA + GDPR ↔ DORA-recommended) are substantively scope-disjoint on the documentation-object dimension, not on the policy-object dimension; the high-level SO does **NOT** engage on these pairs because no same-event overlap exists, but this is **not** an emergent tension requiring Phase 1C resolution.

The **DORA-absence flag (CRDA-deep §D-09.4 line 29)** is the structurally significant operational divergence — DORA is fully absent for RoPA-equivalent (no analogue to GDPR Art. 30); the DORA Art. 9(4)(a) information security policy is a **different record type** serving a **different audit purpose**. For DORA financial entities, the GDPR RoPA remains the dedicated authority for processing-activities documentation; the DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation is a separate artefact (cross to D-09.1 DORA sub-SO). Phase 1 entities with GDPR + DORA applicability must maintain both GDPR RoPA + DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation on distinct artefacts that are not substitutable.

The **no OJ-level consolidation rule for documentation flag (CRDA-deep §D-09.4 line 392)** is the structurally significant operational divergence — unlike D-09.2 where CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 permits AI Act Art. 9 risk-management to substitute for CRA risk assessment (the only OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule in D-09.2), the documentation object at D-09.4 has **no OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule**. Phase 1 entities with ≥2 of the 5 participating regs applicability must maintain **parallel documentation regimes** even when the underlying product is CRA-conformant and the underlying AI system is AI-Act high-risk. **No cross-substitution is OJ-permitted**.

The **3 substantively scope-disjoint pairs (GDPR ↔ NIS 2 + GDPR ↔ CRA + GDPR ↔ DORA-recommended)** are the structurally significant operational divergence — these 3 pairs operate on **different record types** (RoPA vs. certification-driven records + CRA Annex VII 8-item technical documentation + DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation) serving **different audit purposes** (DPA audit on processing activities vs. Member-State-driven certification audit + MSA audit on product cybersecurity + NCA audit on IS policy). CRDA `different-perspective` is a taxonomy artifact (per CRDA-deep §D-09.4 lines 68, 110, 153) for these three pairs. Phase 1 entities with GDPR + CRA + NIS 2 applicability must maintain **three separate documentation regimes** (GDPR RoPA + CRA Annex VII technical documentation + NIS 2 Art. 24 certification-driven records on distinct artefacts serving distinct audit purposes).

The **5 parallel documentation regimes** are the structurally significant compliance-architecture: **GDPR Art. 30 RoPA on processing activities** + **NIS 2 Art. 24 certification-driven records** + **CRA Annex VII 8-item technical documentation + EU declaration + CE marking** + **DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation** + **AI Act Annex IV + Art. 17(1) QMS + Art. 19(1) logs**. The 5 regimes operate in parallel with distinct record types, distinct triggers, distinct retention cadences, and distinct recipients. **No OJ-level cross-substitution exists** between any pair. Phase 1 entities with all 5 participating regs applicability (a DORA financial entity that is also a controller + NSIE + manufacturer + high-risk-AI provider) must maintain **5 parallel documentation regimes** serving 5 distinct audit purposes.

The **CRA Art. 27(8)/(9) CSA-certification presumption-of-conformity machinery** is the **only cross-regulation link at D-09.4 involving CRA + NIS 2** — when an NSIE procures CSA-certified CRA products, the CRA Art. 27(8)/(9) `A product with digital elements and the processes put in place by the manufacturer for which the manufacturer has obtained an EU statement of conformity or an EU certificate under a European cybersecurity certification scheme adopted pursuant to Regulation (EU) 2019/881, at assurance level at least 'substantial', shall be presumed to be in conformity with the essential cybersecurity requirements` is the upstream gate for NIS 2 procurements.

The high-level SO surfaces the **common documentation-regime duty** (records of processing activities + technical documentation + certification records + IS-policy documentation + QMS documentation + provider logs) on **parallel artefacts** serving distinct audit purposes; the per-reg perspective on the artefact, anchor, governance body, content template, retention cadence, recipient, and evidence template is documented separately in the 5 sub-SOs and the per-recipient evidence packs. **0 conditional emergent tensions** because no pair was verified as CONTRADICTORY or CORRECTED — the 5-distinct-documentation-regimes structure (GDPR Art. 30 RoPA + NIS 2 Art. 24 + CRA Annex VII 8-item + DORA Art. 9(4)(a) + AI Act Annex IV + Art. 17(1) QMS + Art. 19(1) logs) with **DORA-absence flag + no OJ-level consolidation rule for documentation + 3 substantively scope-disjoint pairs (GDPR ↔ NIS 2 / CRA / DORA-recommended)** is itself the structurally significant compliance-architecture that supersedes any single-regulator reading.

#### Downstream implication for Phase 1C Doc 07

A company with ≥2 applicable regulations in scope of D-09.4 — with overlapping documentation scope-determiners on the same party — operates **5 parallel documentation regimes** (GDPR Art. 30 RoPA + NIS 2 Art. 24 + CRA Annex VII 8-item technical documentation + DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation + AI Act Annex IV + Art. 17(1) QMS + Art. 19(1) logs) on **distinct artefacts** serving **distinct audit purposes**. The high-level SO (`SO-D-09.4.HL`) describes the **common documentation-regime duty** (records of processing activities + technical documentation + certification records + IS-policy documentation + QMS documentation + provider logs) on parallel artefacts; the per-reg perspective is preserved in the 5 sub-SOs (artefact, governance body, anchor, content template, retention cadence, recipient, evidence template). **The 0 conditional emergent tensions are documented as a layered reconciliation**, with the 5-parallel-documentation-regimes operationalising the per-reg governance body on the integrated documentation architecture. The high-level SO does NOT collapse the 5 documentation regimes into a single artefact; the per-reg perspective on the artefact, governance body, anchor, content template, retention cadence, recipient, and evidence template is preserved verbatim. Phase 1C Doc 07 documents the **documentation-architecture strategy**: (1) a **5-parallel-documentation-regimes architecture** with explicit governance-body hand-offs (GDPR Art. 30(1) RoPA + Art. 12-14 transparency → NIS 2 Art. 24(1)/(2)/(3) certification-driven records → CRA Annex VII §1-§8 8-item technical documentation + Art. 13(12)/(13)/(15)/(16)/(17)/(19)/(20)/(21)/(22)/(23)/EU declaration/CE marking → DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation → AI Act Annex IV + Art. 17(1) QMS + Art. 19(1) logs); (2) the **DORA-absence flag** preserving GDPR RoPA as the dedicated authority for processing-activities documentation even for DORA financial entities (GDPR Art. 30 RoPA + DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation on parallel artefacts serving distinct audit purposes); (3) the **no OJ-level consolidation rule for documentation** distinguishing D-09.4 from D-09.2 (the CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 risk-assessment consolidation rule covers the *risk-assessment object* but not the *documentation object* at D-09.4); (4) the **3 substantively scope-disjoint pairs** (GDPR ↔ NIS 2 + GDPR ↔ CRA + GDPR ↔ DORA-recommended) preserving GDPR RoPA on processing activities from cross-substitution by NIS 2 Art. 24 + CRA Annex VII + DORA Art. 9(4)(a); (5) the **CRA Art. 27(8)/(9) CSA-certification presumption-of-conformity machinery** as the only cross-regulation link at D-09.4 involving CRA + NIS 2 — when an NSIE procures CSA-certified CRA products, the CRA Art. 27(8)/(9) is the upstream gate for NIS 2 procurements (Conditional-Y scope overlap); (6) the **5 distinct retention cadences** (GDPR until processing ceases + on-DPA-request; CRA 10 years or support period, whichever longer; AI Act no OJ-quantified retention for documentation; DORA no OJ-quantified retention; NIS 2 per Member State) — Phase 1C Doc 07 must perform the Layer 1 cross-cutting retention-cadence reconciliation; (7) the **CRDA NOT-CLASSIFIED pairs** (GDPR ↔ DORA, NIS 2 ↔ DORA, NIS 2 ↔ AI Act, CRA ↔ DORA, DORA ↔ AI Act) — 4 of 5 are recommended-COMPLEMENTARY + 1 is recommended-DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE per CRDA explicit Downstream-implication lines; Phase 1C Doc 07 documents the recommended classifications as the operational reading; (8) the **convergent NIST CSF 2.0 Subcategories** ID.AM-08 + PR.DS-12 anchor the data-lifecycle + risk-strategy consistency as the common documentation objective; GDPR additionally maps GV.PO-02 for demonstrable-compliance; CRA additionally maps ID.RA-05 for risk-assessment documentation.

### 3. Security Requirements

## 9.4 Records of Processing — Volere requirements

> **Provenance.** Derived from `../../HierarchicalSecurityObjectives/D-09_Governance-Documentation/9.4.md`. See that file for the CRDA-deep verified classification (10 pairs: 2 SAME — DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE — GDPR ↔ NIS 2 + GDPR ↔ CRA — substantively scope-disjoint per CRDA `N (typically)`, different record types; + 3 SAME — COMPLEMENTARY classified by CRDA — GDPR ↔ AI Act, NIS 2 ↔ CRA, CRA ↔ AI Act; + 1 SAME — DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE RECOMMENDED-by-CRDA — GDPR ↔ DORA substantively scope-disjoint; + 4 SAME — COMPLEMENTARY RECOMMENDED-by-CRDA — NIS 2 ↔ DORA, NIS 2 ↔ AI Act, CRA ↔ DORA, DORA ↔ AI Act) — no CORRECTED, no CONTRADICTORY; **5 of 5 regulations participate**; the **DORA-absence flag (CRDA-deep §9.4 line 29)** — DORA is fully absent for RoPA-equivalent (no GDPR Art. 30 analogue); DORA Art. 9(4)(a) information security policy is a different record type serving a different audit purpose (NCA audit on IS policy vs. DPA audit on processing activities); for DORA financial entities, the GDPR RoPA remains the dedicated authority for processing-activities documentation; **no OJ-level consolidation rule for documentation at 9.4** (the CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 risk-assessment consolidation rule at 9.2 covers the *risk-assessment object* not the *documentation object*); the per-reg 5-distinct-documentation-artefact architecture (GDPR Art. 30(1)/(2)/(3)/(4)/(5) RoPA + Art. 12-14 transparency; NIS 2 Art. 24(1)/(2)/(3) certification-driven records per Member State; CRA Annex VII §1-§8 8-item technical documentation + Art. 13(12)-(25)/(28)/(30)/(31)/(32) + 10-year or support-period retention; DORA Art. 9(4)(a) information security policy — different record type from RoPA; AI Act Art. 11 + Art. 12(1)/(2) + Art. 17(1) 13-element QMS + Art. 19(1) provider logs), the 3 substantively scope-disjoint pairs (GDPR ↔ NIS 2 / CRA / DORA-recommended) operating on different record types serving different audit purposes, the NIS 2 ↔ CRA CSA-certification presumption-of-conformity link via CRA Art. 27(8)/(9), the 5 parallel documentation regimes on distinct artefacts (no cross-substitution OJ-permitted), the per-reg documentation artefact / governance body / anchor / content template / threshold / retention cadence / recipient / evidence template, and the aggregation logic. This file adds Volere shell fields per the `../Volere_shell.md` template.

### Participating regulations

GDPR, NIS 2, CRA, DORA, AI Act (ALL 5 participate on the documentation object). The five regulators each impose a **distinct documentation regime** under a **distinct anchor** at a **distinct trigger** with a **distinct retention cadence** and serving a **distinct audit purpose**: GDPR (Art. 30(1) `record of processing activities under its responsibility` + Art. 30(1) (a)-(g) 7-item content + Art. 30(2) processor RoPA + Art. 30(3) `in writing, including in electronic form` + Art. 30(4) on-supervisory-authority-request + Art. 30(5) 250-employee exception + Art. 12-14 transparency to data subjects + Art. 5(2) accountability burden) — controller/processor RoPA mandating the 7-item content; DPA audit; substantively scope-disjoint from NIS 2 + CRA per CRDA `N (typically)`; NIS 2 (Art. 24(1) sentence 1 `Member States shall encourage the use of European or internationally accepted standards and specifications relevant to the security of network and information systems` + Art. 24(1) sentence 2 qualified trust services encouragement + Art. 24(2) Commission delegated acts + Art. 24(3) ENISA candidate scheme request) — certification-driven recordkeeping per Member State; not codified at OJ level for content — entity-discretionary under Member State transposition; substantively scope-disjoint from GDPR per CRDA `N (typically)`; CRA (Art. 13(12) sentence 1 `the manufacturer shall draw up the technical documentation referred to in Article 31` + Art. 13(12) sentence 2 EU declaration + CE marking + Art. 13(13) `10 years … or for the support period, whichever is longer` + Annex VII §1-§8 8-item content list + Art. 13(15)/(16)/(17)/(19)/(20)/(21)/(22)/(23)/(24)/(25) + Art. 28 EU declaration + Art. 30 CE marking + Art. 31 drawing-up-before-placing + Art. 32 conformity-assessment procedure selection) — product-level technical documentation; MSA audit; substantively scope-disjoint from GDPR per CRDA `N (typically)`; DORA (Art. 9(4)(a) `Financial entities shall … develop and document an information security policy defining rules to protect the availability, authenticity, integrity and confidentiality of data, information assets and ICT assets, including those of their customers, where applicable` + Art. 6(4) three-lines-of-defence + Art. 5(2) management-body oversight) — entity-level IS-policy documentation; NCA audit; **DORA is fully absent for RoPA-equivalent per CRDA explicit note**; AI Act (Art. 11 `technical documentation … in accordance with Annex IV` + Art. 12(1) automatic recording + Art. 12(2) traceability + Art. 17(1) `systematic and orderly manner in the form of written policies, procedures and instructions` 13-element closed list (a)-(m) + Art. 19(1) `Providers of high-risk AI systems shall keep the logs referred to in Article 12(1) … to the extent that such logs are under their control`) — AI-system-specific technical documentation + 13-element QMS + provider logs; MSA + AI Office audit.

---

### High-level requirement

```yaml
- req_id: 9.4
  volere_section: 15
  volere_subtype: audit
  title: "Records of processing activities established through 5 parallel documentation regimes on distinct artefacts serving distinct audit purposes, with DORA fully absent for RoPA-equivalent and no OJ-level cross-substitution permitted"

  derives_from:
    hso_sub_so: SO-9.4.HL
    hso_file: ../../HierarchicalSecurityObjectives/D-09_Governance-Documentation/9.4.md
    objective_ref_section: "### 9.4.0"

  description: |
    The product shall establish records of processing activities through 5 parallel documentation regimes on distinct artefacts serving distinct audit purposes (GDPR Art. 30 RoPA + NIS 2 Art. 24 certification-driven records + CRA Annex VII 8-item technical documentation + DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation + AI Act Annex IV + Art. 17(1) QMS + Art. 19(1) logs), where ≥2 of the participating regulations (GDPR, NIS 2, CRA, DORA, AI Act) have applicability with overlapping documentation scope-determiners on the same party, with the per-reg documentation artefact, governance body, anchor, content template, threshold, retention cadence, recipient, and evidence template preserved verbatim in the 5 sub-SOs, with DORA-absence flag preserved (GDPR RoPA remains the dedicated authority for processing-activities documentation even for DORA financial entities), and with no OJ-permitted cross-substitution between any pair.

  fit_criterion: |
    A 5-parallel-documentation-regimes architecture is established with explicit governance-body hand-offs at the documentation-evidence layer: (1) **GDPR Art. 30(1)/(2)/(3)/(4)/(5) RoPA** — controller/processor record of processing activities with the Art. 30(1) (a)-(g) 7-item content list (name/contact details of controller; purposes; categories of data subjects and personal data; categories of recipients; transfers to third country; envisaged time limits for erasure; general description of TOMs), in writing or electronic form per Art. 30(3), made available to the DPA on request per Art. 30(4), subject to the Art. 30(5) 250-employee exception; the DPA audit channel; substantively scope-disjoint from NIS 2 / CRA per CRDA `N (typically)`; (2) **NIS 2 Art. 24(1)/(2)/(3) certification-driven records** — Member-State-driven use of European or internationally accepted standards + qualified trust services encouragement + Commission delegated acts + ENISA candidate scheme request; entity-discretionary under Member State transposition; substantively scope-disjoint from GDPR per CRDA `N (typically)`; (3) **CRA Annex VII §1-§8 8-item technical documentation** — general description (§1) + design, development, production, vulnerability-handling processes (§2) + cybersecurity risk assessment (§3) + support-period determination (§4) + harmonised standards applied (§5) + reports of tests carried out (§6) + copy of EU declaration of conformity (§7) + SBOM on MSA reasoned request (§8); drawn up before placing on market per Art. 31(2); retained at MSA disposal for at least 10 years or the support period, whichever longer, per Art. 13(13); substantively scope-disjoint from GDPR per CRDA `N (typically)`; (4) **DORA Art. 9(4)(a) information security policy** — entity-level IS-policy defining rules to protect availability, authenticity, integrity and confidentiality of data, information assets and ICT assets including those of customers where applicable; **different record type from GDPR RoPA**; DORA-absence flag preserved (per CRDA-deep §9.4 line 29, DORA is fully absent for RoPA-equivalent; for DORA financial entities, the GDPR RoPA remains the dedicated authority for processing-activities documentation); (5) **AI Act Art. 11 + Art. 12(1)/(2) + Art. 17(1) + Art. 19(1)** — Annex IV technical documentation (per Art. 12(2) traceability) + Art. 17(1) 13-element QMS closed list (a)-(m) in systematic-and-orderly manner + Art. 19(1) provider logs under their control; MSA + AI Office audit. The 5 regimes operate in parallel with distinct record types, distinct triggers, distinct retention cadences, and distinct recipients. **No OJ-permitted cross-substitution exists** between any pair — the CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 risk-assessment consolidation rule at 9.2 covers the *risk-assessment object* not the *documentation object*. Verified by per-recipient evidence packs (DPA RoPA, Member State/competent authority certification records, MSA Annex VII technical file, NCA IS-policy documentation, MSA + AI Office Annex IV + QMS + logs) + automated artefact-distinctness test (no field-level overlap between RoPA Art. 30(1)(a)-(g) and Annex VII §1-§8 content lists; no field-level overlap between DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy and GDPR Art. 30(1) RoPA).

  priority: MUST
  verification_method: TEST
  dependencies: [9.4.1, 9.4.2, 9.4.3, 9.4.4, 9.4.5]

  nist_csf: [ID.AM-08, PR.DS-12, GV.PO-02]

  applicable_if:
    regs: [GDPR, NIS2, CRA, DORA, AI_Act]
    scope_overlap: Conditional   # HL engages when ≥2 applicable regs have ≥1 Y or Conditional-Y-scope-overlap pair on the same party; for 9.4 the matrix shows 7 pairs with Y or Conditional-Y activation on the joint-condition operator (GDPR↔AI_Act = Conditional-Y when provider/deployer is controller; NIS2↔CRA = Conditional-Y when NSIE procures CSA-certified CRA products; NIS2↔DORA = Conditional-Y when DORA financial entity is also NSIE; NIS2↔AI_Act = Conditional-Y when NSIE deploys high-risk AI; CRA↔DORA = Conditional-Y when DORA entity procures CRA-conformant products; CRA↔AI_Act = Y when CRA-regulated product is also high-risk AI; DORA↔AI_Act = Conditional-Y when DORA financial entity deploys high-risk AI). The 3 substantively scope-disjoint pairs (GDPR↔NIS 2, GDPR↔CRA, GDPR↔DORA-recommended) are `N (typically)` — different record types serving different audit purposes; the HL does NOT engage on these pairs alone (no same-event overlap). The HL engages on the 7 same-event activation pairs and surfaces the common documentation-regime duty on parallel artefacts; the per-reg anchor, governance body, content template, threshold, retention cadence, recipient, and evidence template is preserved verbatim in the 5 sub-SOs.

  satisfaction:
    controls: []
    nodes: []

  considerations: |
    The high-level SO surfaces the **common documentation-regime duty** (records of processing activities + technical documentation + certification records + IS-policy documentation + QMS documentation + provider logs) on **parallel artefacts** serving **distinct audit purposes**. The 10 verified pairs are all SAME — 2 SAME — DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE (GDPR ↔ NIS 2 + GDPR ↔ CRA — substantively scope-disjoint per CRDA `N (typically)`, different record types, different audit purposes; CRDA `different-perspective` is a taxonomy artifact for these three pairs) + 3 SAME — COMPLEMENTARY classified by CRDA + 1 SAME — DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE RECOMMENDED-by-CRDA (GDPR ↔ DORA) + 4 SAME — COMPLEMENTARY RECOMMENDED-by-CRDA. No CORRECTED, no CONTRADICTORY. **DORA-absence flag (CRDA-deep §9.4 line 29)** distinguishes 9.4 from the other D-09 sub-domains — DORA does NOT impose a RoPA-equivalent; the DORA Art. 9(4)(a) information security policy is a different record type (entity-level IS-policy documentation) serving a different audit purpose (NCA audit on IS policy) than the GDPR RoPA (DPA audit on processing activities). For DORA financial entities, the GDPR RoPA remains the dedicated authority for processing-activities documentation. **No OJ-level consolidation rule for documentation at 9.4** — unlike 9.2 where CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 permits AI Act Art. 9 risk-management to substitute for CRA risk assessment, that rule covers the *risk-assessment object* not the *documentation object*. The 5 documentation regimes operate in parallel; no OJ-permitted cross-substitution exists between any pair. Phase 1 entities with ≥2 of the 5 participating regs applicability must maintain **parallel documentation regimes** even when the underlying product is CRA-conformant and the underlying AI system is AI-Act high-risk. The **3 substantively scope-disjoint pairs (GDPR ↔ NIS 2 + GDPR ↔ CRA + GDPR ↔ DORA-recommended)** never engage the HL together — different record types serving different audit purposes; CRDA `different-perspective` is a taxonomy artifact for these three pairs. The **NIS 2 ↔ CRA CSA-certification link** is the only cross-regulation link at 9.4 involving CRA + NIS 2 — when an NSIE procures CSA-certified CRA products, CRA Art. 27(8)/(9) `A product with digital elements and the processes put in place by the manufacturer for which the manufacturer has obtained an EU statement of conformity or an EU certificate under a European cybersecurity certification scheme adopted pursuant to Regulation (EU) 2019/881, at assurance level at least 'substantial', shall be presumed to be in conformity with the essential cybersecurity requirements` is the upstream gate for NIS 2 procurements (certification boundary). The HL does NOT collapse the 5 documentation regimes into a single artefact; the per-reg perspective on the artefact, anchor, governance body, content template, threshold, retention cadence, recipient, and evidence template is preserved verbatim. For a DORA financial entity that is also a controller + NSIE + manufacturer + high-risk-AI provider (all 5 participating regs applicable), the 5 regimes must all be maintained in parallel serving 5 distinct audit purposes.
```

### Sub-requirements

#### 9.4.1 — Controller/processor records of processing activities (RoPA) with the Art. 30(1) 7-item content list, in writing/electronic form, on-supervisory-authority-request

```yaml
- req_id: 9.4.1
  volere_section: 15
  volere_subtype: audit

  title: "Controller and processor maintain a record of processing activities (RoPA) under Art. 30(1)/(2)/(3)/(4)/(5) GDPR with the 7-item content list, on-DPA-request, subject to the 250-employee exception"

  derives_from:
    hso_sub_so: SO-9.4.GDPR
    per_reg_so: SO-GDPR-029 + SO-GDPR-038
    source_sr: SR-GDPR-048 + SR-GDPR-050 + SR-GDPR-065 + SR-GDPR-066 + SR-GDPR-067 + SR-GDPR-068
    hso_file: ../../HierarchicalSecurityObjectives/D-09_Governance-Documentation/9.4.md
    objective_ref_section: "### 9.4.1"

  description: |
    The product shall maintain a record of processing activities under the controller's or processor's responsibility (per GDPR Art. 30(1) + Art. 30(2) processor RoPA), in writing or electronic form (per Art. 30(3) `in writing, including in electronic form`), with the Art. 30(1) (a)-(g) 7-item content list (name/contact details of controller; purposes of processing; categories of data subjects and personal data; categories of recipients; transfers to third country; envisaged time limits for erasure; general description of technical and organisational security measures), made available to the supervisory authority on request (per Art. 30(4)), subject to the Art. 30(5) 250-employee exception, and operationalising the Art. 5(2) accountability burden (per Art. 12-14 transparency to data subjects).

  fit_criterion: |
    A RoPA is maintained per Art. 30(1) `record of processing activities under its responsibility` with the 7-item content list (SR-GDPR-048) verified by ID.AM-08 lifecycle-management dimension (controller demonstrates ex post that systems, hardware, software, services, and data are managed throughout their life cycle — the RoPA records the processing activities supporting that life cycle per CRDA DomainAnalysis §9.4 line 113 cross-validation) + PR.DS-12 risk-strategy-consistent data management (the Art. 30(1)(g) `general description of the technical and organisational security measures` is consistent with the entity's risk strategy) + GV.PO-02 process-and-procedure demonstrable-compliance (the Art. 30(3) `in writing, including in electronic form` form-factor is documented and demonstrable to the DPA on request); the processor maintains a parallel record per Art. 30(2) `record of processing activities carried out on behalf of the controller` with the (a)-(e) content (name/contact details of processor + controller; categories of processing; transfers to third country; TOMs; audit enablement to the extent necessary); records are made available to the supervisory authority on request per Art. 30(4) (SR-GDPR-050); the Art. 30(5) 250-employee exception is read at the R1 literal — `an undertaking or organisation employing fewer than 250 persons` is exempt from the RoPA obligation UNLESS processing is likely to result in a risk to the rights and freedoms of data subjects, the processing is not occasional, OR the processing includes special categories of data or criminal-conviction data; the RoPA operationalises Art. 5(2) accountability burden with Art. 12-14 transparency to data subjects cross-referenced (SR-GDPR-065-068); the recipient is the **DPA (supervisory authority)** on the Art. 30(1) RoPA content items + Art. 30(4) on-DPA-request + Art. 30(5) 250-employee exception + Art. 12-14 transparency; verified by DPA audit pack (RoPA extract, on-DPA-request retrieval procedure, accountability burden evidence, Art. 5(2) continuous compliance evidence) + automated RoPA field-completeness test (all 7 Art. 30(1)(a)-(g) fields populated for each processing activity) + Art. 30(3) form-factor inspection (writing or electronic form). **IMPORTANT — DORA-absence flag**: per CRDA-deep §9.4 line 29, DORA is fully absent for RoPA-equivalent — for DORA financial entities, the GDPR RoPA remains the dedicated authority for processing-activities documentation. The GDPR RoPA does NOT substitute for the CRA Annex VII 8-item technical documentation (substantively scope-disjoint per CRDA `N (typically)`), the NIS 2 Art. 24 certification-driven records (substantively scope-disjoint per CRDA `N (typically)`), the DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation (different record type, different audit purpose), or the AI Act Annex IV + Art. 17(1) QMS documentation (parallel artefacts; no OJ-level consolidation rule for documentation at 9.4).

  priority: MUST
  verification_method: TEST
  dependencies: [9.4.2 (when controller = NSIE — RoPA + certification-driven records on distinct artefacts; substantively scope-disjoint per CRDA `N (typically)`), 9.4.3 (when controller = manufacturer — RoPA + Annex VII 8-item technical documentation on distinct artefacts; substantively scope-disjoint per CRDA `N (typically)`), 9.4.4 (when DORA-controller — DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy is different record type from RoPA; for DORA financial entities, the GDPR RoPA remains the dedicated authority for processing-activities documentation per CRDA-absence flag), 9.4.5 (when provider/deployer = controller — RoPA + AI Act Annex IV + Art. 17 QMS on three parallel artefacts; AI Act Recital 78 explicit GDPR preservation; no OJ-level consolidation rule for documentation)]

  nist_csf: [ID.AM-08, PR.DS-12, GV.PO-02]

  applicable_if:
    regs: [GDPR]
    scope_overlap: Conditional   # GDPR↔NIS2 = N (typically) — substantively scope-disjoint (RoPA vs. certification-driven records; different record types, different audit purposes; CRDA `different-perspective` is a taxonomy artifact); GDPR↔CRA = N (typically) — substantively scope-disjoint (RoPA vs. CRA Annex VII 8-item technical documentation; different record types, different audit purposes); GDPR↔DORA = N (typically) RECOMMENDED — substantively scope-disjoint (RoPA vs. DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation; DORA fully absent for RoPA per CRDA explicit note); GDPR↔AI_Act = Conditional (Y when provider/deployer is controller processing personal data via high-risk AI); HL engages when the GDPR sub-SO activates AND ≥1 other sub-SO activates on Conditional-Y overlap.

  satisfaction:
    controls: []
    nodes: []

  considerations: |
    The GDPR records-of-processing obligation at 9.4 is the **controller/processor RoPA** with **DPO as governance anchor** under Art. 30(1) + Art. 30(2) + Art. 30(3) + Art. 30(4) + Art. 30(5) + Art. 5(2) accountability + Art. 12-14 transparency to data subjects. Art. 30(1) `record of processing activities under its responsibility` (per SR-GDPR-048); Art. 30(1) (a)-(g) 7-item content list is the formal codification — `(a) name and contact details of the controller, any joint controller, the controller's representative and the data protection officer; (b) purposes of the processing; (c) a description of the categories of data subjects and of the categories of personal data; (d) the categories of recipients to whom personal data have been or will be disclosed including recipients in third countries or international organisations; (e) transfers of personal data to a third country or an international organisation …; (f) where possible, the envisaged time limits for erasure of the different categories of data; (g) where possible, a general description of the technical and organisational security measures`. Art. 30(2) processor RoPA content — `the name and contact details of the processor or processors, of each controller on behalf of which the processor is acting, the categories of processing carried out on behalf of the controller, transfers to a third country … and the technical and organisational security measures`. Art. 30(3) `in writing, including in electronic form` — entity-discretionary on form factor. Art. 30(4) on-DPA-request — the records are made available to the supervisory authority on request. Art. 30(5) 250-employee exception — `an undertaking or organisation employing fewer than 250 persons is not obliged to keep records of processing activities, unless … (a) the processing is likely to result in a risk to the rights and freedoms of data subjects; (b) the processing is not occasional; or (c) the processing includes special categories of data as referred to in Article 9(1) or personal data relating to criminal convictions and offences`. Art. 12-14 transparency to data subjects — the RoPA is upstream of the data-subject-facing privacy notice (cross to 9.1 GDPR sub-SO). **DORA-absence flag** (per CRDA-deep §9.4 line 29): for DORA financial entities, the GDPR RoPA remains the dedicated authority for processing-activities documentation; the DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation is a separate artefact. The recipient is the **DPA (supervisory authority)** on the Art. 30(1) RoPA content items + Art. 30(4) on-DPA-request + Art. 30(5) 250-employee exception + Art. 12-14 transparency.
```

#### 9.4.2 — Member-State-driven certification-driven recordkeeping under Art. 24(1)/(2)/(3) for certified ICT products, services, and processes

```yaml
- req_id: 9.4.2
  volere_section: 15
  volere_subtype: audit

  title: "Member States encourage use of European or internationally accepted standards and specifications for the security of network and information systems, with Commission delegated acts under Art. 24(2) and ENISA candidate scheme request under Art. 24(3)"

  derives_from:
    hso_sub_so: SO-9.4.NIS2
    per_reg_so: SO-NIS2-024
    source_sr: SR-NIS2-043
    hso_file: ../../HierarchicalSecurityObjectives/D-09_Governance-Documentation/9.4.md
    objective_ref_section: "### 9.4.2"

  description: |
    The product shall record the use of certified ICT products, ICT services, and ICT processes under Member-State-driven standards and specifications relevant to the security of network and information systems (per NIS 2 Art. 24(1) sentence 1) and of qualified trust services (per Art. 24(1) sentence 2), with the Commission empowered to adopt delegated acts specifying entities required to use certified ICT products/services/processes (per Art. 24(2)) and ENISA empowered to be requested to prepare a candidate European cybersecurity certification scheme (per Art. 24(3)).

  fit_criterion: |
    Certification-driven records are maintained per Art. 24(1) sentence 1 `Member States shall encourage the use of European or internationally accepted standards and specifications relevant to the security of network and information systems` (SR-NIS2-043) verified by ID.AM-08 lifecycle-management dimension (the NSIE demonstrates ex post that certified ICT products/services/processes are inventoried and managed throughout their life cycle per CRDA DomainAnalysis §9.4 line 113 cross-validation) + PR.DS-12 risk-strategy-consistent data management (the certification-driven records are consistent with the entity's risk strategy); Art. 24(1) sentence 2 `Member States shall also encourage, without prejudice to paragraph 1, the use of any qualified trust service` extends the certification-driven records to qualified trust services; Art. 24(2) `The Commission may adopt delegated acts … to specify which categories of essential and important entities shall be required to use certain certified ICT products, ICT services and ICT processes` — the certification-driven records are conditional on the Commission adopting such delegated acts; Art. 24(3) `ENISA may be requested to prepare a candidate European cybersecurity certification scheme` — the certification-driven records may feed ENISA candidate scheme preparation. The content is **not codified at OJ level** — entity-discretionary under Member State transposition. The recipient is the **management body + competent authority (national NIS 2 supervisory authority)** on the Art. 24(1)/(2)/(3) certification-driven records per Member State. **IMPORTANT — NIS 2 partial coverage at 9.4**: the NIS 2 Art. 24(1)/(2)/(3) certification-driven recordkeeping is **partial coverage** — Member-State-driven use of certified ICT products/services/processes, not a RoPA-equivalent; substantively scope-disjoint from GDPR per CRDA `N (typically)` (different record types, different triggers, different audit purposes). Phase 1 entities with NIS 2 + GDPR applicability must maintain both GDPR Art. 30 RoPA + NIS 2 Art. 24 certification-driven records on distinct artefacts that are not substitutable. For NSIEs that are CRA-conformant product procurers, the CRA Art. 27(8)/(9) CSA-certification presumption-of-conformity machinery is the upstream gate for NIS 2 procurements — Phase 1 must verify that procured CRA products hold CSA certification at the assurance level required by the Commission's delegated act under Art. 24(2). The **NIS 2 → CRA certification link** is the only cross-regulation link at 9.4 involving NIS 2.

  priority: MUST
  verification_method: TEST
  dependencies: [9.4.1 (when controller = NSIE — RoPA + certification-driven records on distinct artefacts; substantively scope-disjoint per CRDA `N (typically)`; CRDA `different-perspective` is a taxonomy artifact), 9.4.3 (when NSIE procures CSA-certified CRA products — CRA Art. 27(8)/(9) CSA-certification presumption-of-conformity machinery is the upstream gate for NIS 2 procurements; certification boundary), 9.4.4 (when DORA-NSIE — DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation + NIS 2 Art. 24 certification-driven records on distinct artefacts; CRDA-recommended COMPLEMENTARY), 9.4.5 (when NSIE deploys high-risk AI — NIS 2 Art. 24 certification-driven records + AI Act Art. 17(1) QMS documentation on distinct artefacts; CRDA-recommended COMPLEMENTARY)]

  nist_csf: [ID.AM-08, PR.DS-12]

  applicable_if:
    regs: [NIS2]
    scope_overlap: Conditional   # NIS2↔GDPR = N (typically) — substantively scope-disjoint (certification-driven records vs. RoPA; different record types, different audit purposes; CRDA `different-perspective` is a taxonomy artifact); NIS2↔CRA = Conditional (Y when NSIE procures CSA-certified CRA products; certification boundary); NIS2↔DORA = Conditional (Y when DORA financial entity is also NSIE; CRDA-recommended COMPLEMENTARY); NIS2↔AI_Act = Conditional (Y when NSIE deploys high-risk AI; CRDA-recommended COMPLEMENTARY); HL engages when the NIS 2 sub-SO activates AND ≥1 other sub-SO activates on Y or Conditional-Y overlap.

  satisfaction:
    controls: []
    nodes: []

  considerations: |
    The NIS 2 records-of-processing obligation at 9.4 is the **Member-State-driven certification-driven recordkeeping** under Art. 24(1) sentence 1 (European or internationally accepted standards and specifications) + Art. 24(1) sentence 2 (qualified trust services) + Art. 24(2) (Commission delegated acts specifying which categories of essential and important entities must use certified ICT products/services/processes) + Art. 24(3) (ENISA candidate scheme request). The content is **not codified at OJ level** — entity-discretionary under Member State transposition. Art. 24(1) `Member States shall encourage the use of European or internationally accepted standards and specifications relevant to the security of network and information systems` (per SR-NIS2-043) — the `encourage` verb is VAG-3 (Berry §5.1 — no OJ-anchored threshold); the entity-discretionary reading is dominant since OJ does not enumerate specific record content. The **NIS 2 → CRA certification link** is the only cross-regulation link at 9.4 involving NIS 2 — CRA Art. 27(8) `A product with digital elements and the processes put in place by the manufacturer for which the manufacturer has obtained an EU statement of conformity or an EU certificate under a European cybersecurity certification scheme adopted pursuant to Regulation (EU) 2019/881, at assurance level at least 'substantial', shall be presumed to be in conformity with the essential cybersecurity requirements` + Art. 27(9) provides the **CSA-certification presumption-of-conformity machinery** as the upstream gate for NSIE procurements. The **NIS 2 partial coverage** at 9.4 is structurally significant — substantively scope-disjoint from GDPR per CRDA `N (typically)` (different record types, different triggers, different audit purposes); Phase 1 entities with NIS 2 + GDPR applicability must maintain both GDPR Art. 30 RoPA + NIS 2 Art. 24 certification-driven records on distinct artefacts. The recipient is the **management body + competent authority (national NIS 2 supervisory authority)** on the Art. 24(1)/(2)/(3) certification-driven records per Member State. Member State transposition divergence may apply on documentation expected to evidence the certification-driven records.
```

#### 9.4.3 — Product-level technical documentation per Annex VII §1-§8 8-item content list, 10-year or support-period retention, MSA audit

```yaml
- req_id: 9.4.3
  volere_section: 15
  volere_subtype: audit

  title: "Manufacturer draws up before placing on market the technical documentation per Annex VII §1-§8 8-item content list, keeps it at MSA disposal for at least 10 years or the support period (whichever longer), and provides the EU declaration + CE marking"

  derives_from:
    hso_sub_so: SO-9.4.CRA
    per_reg_so: SO-CRA-055 + SO-CRA-056 + SO-CRA-057 + SO-CRA-058 + SO-CRA-059 + SO-CRA-061 + SO-CRA-062 + SO-CRA-063 + SO-CRA-064 + SO-CRA-065 + SO-CRA-066 + SO-CRA-067
    source_sr: SR-CRA-085 + SR-CRA-087 + SR-CRA-088 + SR-CRA-078
    hso_file: ../../HierarchicalSecurityObjectives/D-09_Governance-Documentation/9.4.md
    objective_ref_section: "### 9.4.3"

  description: |
    The product shall draw up before placing a product with digital elements on the market the technical documentation referred to in Art. 31 (per CRA Art. 13(12) sentence 1), carry out the conformity assessment procedure referred to in Art. 32, draw up the EU declaration of conformity under Art. 28, and affix the CE marking under Art. 30 (per Art. 13(12) sentence 2), with the technical documentation and the EU declaration of conformity kept at the disposal of market surveillance authorities for at least 10 years or the support period, whichever is longer (per Art. 13(13)), and the Annex VII §1-§8 8-item content list (general description; design, development, production, and vulnerability-handling processes; cybersecurity risk assessment; support-period determination; harmonised standards applied; reports of tests carried out; copy of the EU declaration of conformity; SBOM on MSA reasoned request) maintained throughout the support period.

  fit_criterion: |
    Technical documentation is drawn up before placing on market per Art. 13(12) sentence 1 `the manufacturer shall draw up the technical documentation referred to in Article 31` (SR-CRA-085) verified by ID.AM-08 lifecycle-management dimension (manufacturer demonstrates ex post that the Annex VII §1-§8 8-item content list is managed throughout the product life cycle per CRDA DomainAnalysis §9.4 line 113 cross-validation) + PR.DS-12 risk-strategy-consistent data management (the Annex VII §1 `general description` + §3 `cybersecurity risk assessment` dimensions are consistent with the entity's risk strategy per CRDA DomainAnalysis §9.4 line 113) + ID.RA-05 risk-priority-response dimension (the Annex VII §3 `cybersecurity risk assessment` documentation is mapped to the priority risk responses per CRDA DomainAnalysis §9.4 line 113); the Annex VII §1-§8 8-item content list is fully content-codified (§1 general description; §2 design, development, production, and vulnerability-handling processes; §3 cybersecurity risk assessment; §4 support-period determination; §5 harmonised standards applied; §6 reports of tests carried out; §7 copy of the EU declaration of conformity; §8 SBOM on MSA reasoned request — per SR-CRA-087 + SR-CRA-088); the technical documentation is drawn up before placing on the market per Art. 31(2); the manufacturer carries out the conformity assessment procedure referred to in Art. 32 (selection of internal production control per Annex VI or third-party assessment per Annex VII depending on criticality class); the manufacturer draws up the EU declaration of conformity per Art. 28 + CE marking per Art. 30 (per Art. 13(12) sentence 2); the technical documentation + EU declaration of conformity are kept at MSA disposal for at least 10 years or the support period, whichever longer, per Art. 13(13) `10 years … or for the support period, whichever is longer` (SR-CRA-085); the manufacturer is identified per Art. 13(15) (single point of contact per Art. 13(17) + Art. 13(16) for product-identification where applicable); the support-period end-date is displayed per Art. 13(19) (cross to D-06.3 Contractual Security Obligations); the EU declaration is provided per Art. 13(20) + Annex II §6; immediate corrective measures are taken per Art. 13(21); MSA cooperation per Art. 13(22); cessation-of-operations notification per Art. 13(23); Commission implementing/delegated acts per Art. 13(24)/(25) + Art. 14(9)/(10) + Art. 7(3)/(4) + Art. 30(6). The recipient is the **MSA (market surveillance authority)** on the Annex VII §1-§8 8-item content list + Art. 13(12)/(13)/(15)-(25) + Art. 28 EU declaration + Art. 30 CE marking. Verified by MSA technical-file audit pack (Annex VII §1-§8 content list extract, EU declaration copy, CE marking documentation, 10-year or support-period retention evidence, SBOM on MSA reasoned request) + automated Annex VII §1-§8 field-completeness test. **IMPORTANT — CRA full content codification**: CRA Annex VII §1-§8 is fully content-codified — the 8-item content list is explicit (vs GDPR's Art. 30(1)(a)-(g) RoPA content list with entity-discretionary fields such as `where possible`). **IMPORTANT — CRA Annex VII does NOT cross-substitute for GDPR RoPA**: substantively scope-disjoint per CRDA `N (typically)` (different record types, different triggers, different audit purposes). **IMPORTANT — CRA + AI Act for AI-enabled products do NOT have OJ-level consolidation for documentation**: unlike the CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 risk-assessment consolidation rule at 9.2 (which substitutes AI Act Art. 9 risk-management for CRA risk-assessment), no equivalent documentation-level consolidation rule exists at 9.4.

  priority: MUST
  verification_method: TEST
  dependencies: [9.4.1 (when controller-manufacturer — RoPA + Annex VII 8-item technical documentation on distinct artefacts; substantively scope-disjoint per CRDA `N (typically)`; CRDA `different-perspective` is a taxonomy artifact), 9.4.2 (when NSIE procures CSA-certified CRA products — CRA Art. 27(8)/(9) CSA-certification presumption-of-conformity machinery is the upstream gate for NIS 2 procurements; certification boundary), 9.4.4 (when DORA entity procures CRA-conformant products — CRA Annex VII 8-item technical documentation + DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation on distinct artefacts; CRDA-recommended COMPLEMENTARY), 9.4.5 (when CRA-regulated product is also high-risk AI system — CRA Annex VII + AI Act Annex IV + AI Act Art. 17(1) QMS on three parallel artefacts; no OJ-level consolidation rule for documentation at 9.4)]

  nist_csf: [ID.AM-08, PR.DS-12, ID.RA-05]

  applicable_if:
    regs: [CRA]
    scope_overlap: Conditional   # CRA↔GDPR = N (typically) — substantively scope-disjoint (CRA Annex VII 8-item technical documentation vs. RoPA; different record types, different audit purposes; CRDA `different-perspective` is a taxonomy artifact); CRA↔NIS2 = Conditional (Y when NSIE procures CSA-certified CRA products; certification boundary via CRA Art. 27(8)/(9)); CRA↔DORA = Conditional (Y when DORA entity procures CRA-conformant products; CRDA-recommended COMPLEMENTARY); CRA↔AI_Act = Y (CRA-regulated product is also high-risk AI system; CRA Annex VII + AI Act Annex IV + Art. 17(1) QMS on three parallel artefacts; no OJ-level consolidation rule for documentation); HL engages when the CRA sub-SO activates AND ≥1 other sub-SO activates on Y or Conditional-Y overlap.

  satisfaction:
    controls: []
    nodes: []

  considerations: |
    The CRA records-of-processing obligation at 9.4 is the **product-level technical documentation** per Annex VII §1-§8 8-item content list (fully content-codified), with 10-year or support-period retention (whichever longer) per Art. 13(13), drawn up before placing on the market per Art. 31(2) + Art. 13(12) sentence 1, and the manufacturer as governance body per Art. 13. The Annex VII §1-§8 8-item content list is the densest documentation regime of the 5 (covering product identification + manufacturer contact + SPOC + support-period + EU declaration + CE marking + conformity assessment + MSA cooperation + cessation-of-operations + Commission implementing/delegated acts). Art. 13(12) sentence 2 requires the manufacturer to carry out the conformity assessment procedure per Art. 32 + draw up the EU declaration of conformity per Art. 28 + affix the CE marking per Art. 30. Art. 13(13) `10 years … or for the support period, whichever is longer` — the retention period is the maximum of 10 years or the support period. Art. 13(15) + Art. 13(16) + Art. 13(17) cover product identification + manufacturer contact + single point of contact. Art. 13(19) + Art. 13(20) + Art. 13(21) + Art. 13(22) + Art. 13(23) cover support-period end-date display + EU declaration provision + immediate corrective measures + MSA cooperation + cessation-of-operations notification. Art. 13(24) + Art. 13(25) + Art. 14(9) + Art. 14(10) + Art. 7(3) + Art. 7(4) + Art. 30(6) cover Commission implementing/delegated acts. Art. 28 + Art. 30 + Art. 31 + Art. 32 cover EU declaration of conformity + CE marking + technical-documentation drawing-up-before-placing + conformity-assessment procedure selection. **CRA + AI Act for AI-enabled products do NOT have OJ-level consolidation for documentation**: the CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 unification rule at 9.2 covers the *risk-assessment object*; there is no equivalent documentation-level consolidation rule at 9.4; the two regimes maintain parallel artefacts (CRA Annex VII + AI Act Annex IV + AI Act Art. 17(1) QMS). The recipient is the **MSA (market surveillance authority)** on the Annex VII §1-§8 8-item content list + Art. 13(12)-(25) + Art. 28 EU declaration + Art. 30 CE marking.
```

#### 9.4.4 — Entity-level information security policy per Art. 9(4)(a) defining rules to protect availability, authenticity, integrity and confidentiality of data, information assets and ICT assets (different record type from RoPA)

```yaml
- req_id: 9.4.4
  volere_section: 15
  volere_subtype: audit

  title: "Financial entity develops and documents an information security policy defining rules to protect availability, authenticity, integrity and confidentiality of data, information assets and ICT assets, including those of customers where applicable (Art. 9(4)(a) DORA)"

  derives_from:
    hso_sub_so: SO-9.4.DORA
    per_reg_so: SO-DORA-018 (cross to 9.1 DORA sub-SO)
    source_sr: SR-DORA-031
    hso_file: ../../HierarchicalSecurityObjectives/D-09_Governance-Documentation/9.4.md
    objective_ref_section: "### 9.4.4"

  description: |
    The product shall develop and document an information security policy defining rules to protect the availability, authenticity, integrity and confidentiality of data, information assets, and ICT assets, including those of their customers, where applicable (per DORA Art. 9(4)(a)), under the management-body define/approve/oversee/be-responsible architecture per Art. 5(2) four-verb coordination, the three-lines-of-defence model per Art. 6(4), and the proportionality factors per Art. 4 (size, nature, scale, and complexity of the financial entity's activities); this is entity-level IS-policy documentation serving NCA audit purposes and is a **different record type from the GDPR RoPA**.

  fit_criterion: |
    An information security policy is developed and documented per Art. 9(4)(a) `Financial entities shall … develop and document an information security policy defining rules to protect the availability, authenticity, integrity and confidentiality of data, information assets and ICT assets, including those of their customers, where applicable` (SR-DORA-031) verified by ID.AM-08 lifecycle-management dimension (the financial entity demonstrates ex post that the IS-policy is managed throughout the life cycle of data, information assets, and ICT assets per CRDA DomainAnalysis §9.4 line 113 cross-validation) + PR.DS-12 risk-strategy-consistent data management (the IS-policy CIA+A 4-way + customer-where-applicable dimension is consistent with the entity's risk strategy per CRDA DomainAnalysis §9.4 line 113) + GV.PO-01 cybersecurity-policy substrate (the IS-policy is an organisational policy under the management-body define/approve/oversee/be-responsible architecture per Art. 5(2) — cross to 9.1 DORA sub-SO); the management body discharges the Art. 5(2) four-verb coordination `define, approve, oversee and be responsible for` (per SR-DORA-026); the three-lines-of-defence model is operationalised per Art. 6(4) (operational-management as first line, risk-management and compliance as second line, internal-audit as third line); the proportionality factors per Art. 4 `in relation to the size, nature, scale and complexity of the financial entity's activities` calibrate the IS-policy depth; no OJ-quantified retention period — entity-discretionary under Member State transposition; the recipient is the **competent authority (NCA — national financial supervisory authority) + management body** on the Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation serving NCA audit purposes. Verified by NCA audit pack (IS-policy extract, Art. 5(2) management-body approval minute, three-lines-of-defence documentation, Art. 4 proportionality calibration evidence) + automated CIA+A field-completeness test (availability, authenticity, integrity, confidentiality of data + information assets + ICT assets, customer-where-applicable). **IMPORTANT — DORA-absence flag**: per CRDA-deep §9.4 line 29, DORA is fully absent for RoPA-equivalent — DORA does not impose a RoPA obligation (no analogue to GDPR Art. 30). The DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation is a **different record type** (entity-level IS-policy documentation) serving a **different audit purpose** (NCA audit on IS policy) than the GDPR RoPA (DPA audit on processing activities). For DORA financial entities, the GDPR RoPA remains the dedicated authority for processing-activities documentation; the DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation is a separate artefact. **DORA BC/DR logging obligations (Art. 12) are operational continuity artefacts, not RoPA-equivalents** — covered separately under D-10 Monitoring & Audit. Phase 1 entities with GDPR + DORA applicability must maintain both GDPR RoPA + DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation on distinct artefacts that are not substitutable (no cross-substitution OJ-permitted).

  priority: MUST
  verification_method: TEST
  dependencies: [9.4.1 (when DORA-controller — DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy is different record type from RoPA; for DORA financial entities, the GDPR RoPA remains the dedicated authority for processing-activities documentation per CRDA-absence flag; the two artefacts are not substitutable), 9.4.2 (when DORA-NSIE — DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation + NIS 2 Art. 24 certification-driven records on distinct artefacts; CRDA-recommended COMPLEMENTARY), 9.4.3 (when DORA entity procures CRA-conformant products — DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation + CRA Annex VII 8-item technical documentation on distinct artefacts; CRDA-recommended COMPLEMENTARY), 9.4.5 (when DORA financial entity deploys high-risk AI — DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy + AI Act Art. 17(1) QMS on distinct artefacts; CRDA-recommended COMPLEMENTARY)]

  nist_csf: [ID.AM-08, PR.DS-12, GV.PO-01]

  applicable_if:
    regs: [DORA]
    scope_overlap: Conditional   # DORA↔GDPR = N (typically) RECOMMENDED — substantively scope-disjoint (DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation vs. RoPA; DORA fully absent for RoPA per CRDA explicit note; different record types, different audit purposes; CRDA `different-perspective` is a taxonomy artifact); DORA↔NIS2 = Conditional (Y when DORA financial entity is also NSIE; CRDA-recommended COMPLEMENTARY); DORA↔CRA = Conditional (Y when DORA entity procures CRA-conformant products; CRDA-recommended COMPLEMENTARY); DORA↔AI_Act = Conditional (Y when DORA financial entity deploys high-risk AI; CRDA-recommended COMPLEMENTARY); HL engages when the DORA sub-SO activates AND ≥1 other sub-SO activates on Y or Conditional-Y overlap.

  satisfaction:
    controls: []
    nodes: []

  considerations: |
    The DORA records-of-processing obligation at 9.4 is the **entity-level information security policy** per Art. 9(4)(a), with **management-body define/approve/oversee/be-responsible** per Art. 5(2) + **three-lines-of-defence** per Art. 6(4) + **proportionality factors** per Art. 4 as governance anchor. **DORA-absence flag (CRDA-deep §9.4 line 29)**: DORA is fully absent for RoPA-equivalent — DORA does not impose a RoPA obligation (no analogue to GDPR Art. 30). The DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation is a **different record type** (entity-level IS-policy documentation) serving a **different audit purpose** (NCA audit on IS policy) than the GDPR RoPA (DPA audit on processing activities). For DORA financial entities, the GDPR RoPA remains the dedicated authority for processing-activities documentation; the DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation is a separate artefact (cross to 9.1 DORA sub-SO). **DORA BC/DR logging obligations (Art. 12) are operational continuity artefacts, not RoPA-equivalents** — covered separately under D-10 Monitoring & Audit. The CIA+A 4-way `availability, authenticity, integrity and confidentiality` (VAG + COORD per SR-DORA-031 ambiguity_notes) + customer-where-applicable dimension is the IS-policy coverage anchor. The recipient is the **competent authority (NCA — national financial supervisory authority) + management body** on the Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy documentation serving NCA audit purposes. No OJ-quantified retention period for the IS-policy — entity-discretionary under Member State transposition + EBA Guidelines supervisory baseline.
```

#### 9.4.5 — Provider-side Annex IV technical documentation + Art. 17(1) 13-element QMS documentation + Art. 19(1) provider logs (MSA + AI Office audit)

```yaml
- req_id: 9.4.5
  volere_section: 15
  volere_subtype: audit

  title: "Provider of high-risk AI system draws up and keeps up-to-date the Annex IV technical documentation before placing on market or putting into service, the 13-element QMS documentation, and the Art. 19(1) provider logs under their control"

  derives_from:
    hso_sub_so: SO-9.4.AI_Act
    per_reg_so: SO-AIACT-009 + SO-AIACT-010 + SO-AIACT-008
    source_sr: SR-AIACT-008 + SR-AIACT-009 + SR-AIACT-010 + SR-AIACT-017
    hso_file: ../../HierarchicalSecurityObjectives/D-09_Governance-Documentation/9.4.md
    objective_ref_section: "### 9.4.5"

  description: |
    The product shall draw up and keep up-to-date the technical documentation of the high-risk AI system in accordance with Annex IV before that system is placed on the market or put into service (per AI Act Art. 11(1)), keep the logs automatically recorded by the high-risk AI system to the extent under the provider's control (per Art. 19(1) `Providers of high-risk AI systems shall keep the logs referred to in Article 12(1) … to the extent that such logs are under their control`), and document the quality management system in written policies, procedures, and instructions in a systematic and orderly manner covering at least the 13 enumerated aspects (a)-(m) of Art. 17(1) closed list, with AI Act Recital 78 explicit GDPR preservation.

  fit_criterion: |
    Technical documentation is drawn up and kept up-to-date per Art. 11(1) `Providers of high-risk AI systems shall draw up and keep up-to-date the technical documentation of the high-risk AI system in accordance with Annex IV before that system is placed on the market or put into service` (SR-AIACT-009) verified by ID.AM-08 lifecycle-management dimension (the provider demonstrates ex post that the Annex IV technical documentation + Art. 19(1) logs + Art. 17(1) QMS documentation are managed throughout the AI-system life cycle per CRDA DomainAnalysis §9.4 line 113 cross-validation) + PR.DS-12 risk-strategy-consistent data management (the Art. 17(1)(d) `data quality` + Art. 17(1)(g) `risk-management-system documentation cross-reference to Art. 9` are consistent with the entity's risk strategy per CRDA DomainAnalysis §9.4 line 113) + GV.PO-01 cybersecurity-policy substrate (the Art. 17(1) QMS `systematic and orderly manner in the form of written policies, procedures and instructions` is an organisational policy under the QMS — cross to 9.1 AI Act sub-SO); the Annex IV technical documentation is drawn up before placing on the market or putting into service per Art. 11(1); the Art. 19(1) `Providers of high-risk AI systems shall keep the logs referred to in Article 12(1) … to the extent that such logs are under their control` (SR-AIACT-008) covers the automatic recording of events over the high-risk AI system's life cycle per Art. 12(1) + traceability per Art. 12(2); the Art. 17(1) QMS documentation covers the 13 enumerated aspects (a) compliance strategy; (b) design, development, and verification techniques; (c) design, design control, design verification of the high-risk AI system; (d) post-market monitoring (cross to 10.1 AI Act sub-SO); (e) data quality; (f) ... (m) — full content codification (vs GDPR's entity-discretionary content under Art. 24(2) or NIS 2's entity-discretionary content under Recital 56 proportionality); the recipient is the **MSA (market surveillance authority) + AI Office + provider** on the Art. 11 Annex IV technical documentation + Art. 12(1)/(2) automatic recording + Art. 17(1) 13-element QMS + Art. 19(1) logs; verified by MSA + AI Office audit pack (Annex IV technical documentation extract, QMS documentation extract, Art. 19(1) log retention evidence) + automated Annex IV field-completeness test + automated 13-element QMS field-completeness test. **IMPORTANT — AI Act Recital 78 GDPR preservation**: AI Act Recital 78 explicitly preserves GDPR for personal-data processing — `protection of natural persons in relation to the processing of personal data … is governed by Regulation (EU) 2016/679`; providers that are also controllers maintain **parallel artefacts** (GDPR RoPA + AI Act Annex IV + AI Act Art. 17(1) QMS) on distinct artefacts that are not substitutable. **IMPORTANT — CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 risk-assessment consolidation does NOT extend to documentation**: the CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 unification rule at 9.2 covers the *risk-assessment object*; there is no equivalent documentation-level consolidation rule at 9.4; the two regimes maintain **parallel artefacts** (CRA Annex VII + AI Act Annex IV + AI Act Art. 17(1) QMS).

  priority: MUST
  verification_method: TEST
  dependencies: [9.4.1 (when provider/deployer = controller — GDPR RoPA + AI Act Annex IV + AI Act Art. 17(1) QMS on three parallel artefacts; AI Act Recital 78 explicit GDPR preservation; no OJ-level consolidation rule for documentation), 9.4.2 (when NSIE deploys high-risk AI — NIS 2 Art. 24 certification-driven records + AI Act Art. 17(1) QMS on distinct artefacts; CRDA-recommended COMPLEMENTARY), 9.4.3 (when CRA-regulated product is also high-risk AI system — CRA Annex VII + AI Act Annex IV + AI Act Art. 17(1) QMS on three parallel artefacts; no OJ-level consolidation rule for documentation at 9.4 — the CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 risk-assessment consolidation rule at 9.2 covers the *risk-assessment object* not the *documentation object*), 9.4.4 (when DORA financial entity deploys high-risk AI — DORA Art. 9(4)(a) IS-policy + AI Act Art. 17(1) QMS on distinct artefacts; CRDA-recommended COMPLEMENTARY)]

  nist_csf: [ID.AM-08, PR.DS-12, GV.PO-01]

  applicable_if:
    regs: [AI_Act]
    scope_overlap: Conditional   # AI_Act↔GDPR = Conditional (Y when provider/deployer is controller processing personal data via high-risk AI; GDPR RoPA + AI Act Annex IV + Art. 17(1) QMS on three parallel artefacts; AI Act Recital 78 explicit GDPR preservation; no OJ-level consolidation rule for documentation); AI_Act↔NIS2 = Conditional (Y when NSIE deploys high-risk AI; CRDA-recommended COMPLEMENTARY); AI_Act↔CRA = Y (CRA-regulated product is also high-risk AI system; CRA Annex VII + AI Act Annex IV + Art. 17(1) QMS on three parallel artefacts; no OJ-level consolidation rule for documentation at 9.4); AI_Act↔DORA = Conditional (Y when DORA financial entity deploys high-risk AI; CRDA-recommended COMPLEMENTARY); HL engages when the AI Act sub-SO activates AND ≥1 other sub-SO activates on Y or Conditional-Y overlap.

  satisfaction:
    controls: []
    nodes: []

  considerations: |
    The AI Act records-of-processing obligation at 9.4 is the **AI-system-specific technical documentation** (Annex IV via Art. 11/12 traceability) + **QMS documentation of 13 enumerated aspects** (Art. 17(1) `systematic and orderly manner in the form of written policies, procedures and instructions`) + **high-risk-AI-system provider logs** (Art. 19(1)) with the provider as governance body. Art. 11(1) `Providers of high-risk AI systems shall draw up and keep up-to-date the technical documentation of the high-risk AI system in accordance with Annex IV before that system is placed on the market or put into service` (per SR-AIACT-009); Art. 12(1) automatic recording of events + Art. 12(2) traceability; Art. 17(1) 13-element QMS closed list (a)-(m) with the `systematic and orderly manner` formality (VAG-2 per SR-AIACT-017 ambiguity_notes); Art. 19(1) `Providers of high-risk AI systems shall keep the logs referred to in Article 12(1) … to the extent that such logs are under their control` (per SR-AIACT-008) — the `to the extent that such logs are under their control` qualifier carries VAG-3 (Berry §5.1 — OJ does not anchor the extent of provider control; chosen reading: provider control extends to the high-risk AI system itself and the immediate environment under the provider's operational control). **AI Act Recital 78 GDPR preservation**: AI Act Recital 78 explicitly preserves GDPR — `protection of natural persons in relation to the processing of personal data by Union institutions, bodies, offices and agencies … is governed by Regulation (EU) 2018/1725`; explicit Regulation (EU) 2016/679 preservation. **CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 risk-assessment consolidation does NOT extend to documentation**: the CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 unification rule at 9.2 covers the *risk-assessment object*; there is no equivalent documentation-level consolidation rule at 9.4. The 13-element QMS closed list is fully content-codified (vs GDPR's entity-discretionary content under Art. 24(2) or NIS 2's entity-discretionary content under Recital 56 proportionality). The recipient is the **MSA (market surveillance authority) + AI Office + provider** on the Art. 11 Annex IV technical documentation + Art. 12(1)/(2) automatic recording + Art. 17(1) 13-element QMS + Art. 19(1) logs. No OJ-quantified retention period for the technical documentation or QMS — entity-discretionary under Member State transposition + AI Office supervisory baseline.
```

> **Deep analysis:** [D-09.4 Records of Processing](../../DeepAnalysis/D-09_Governance-Documentation/D-09.4.md)

#### D-09.4 Records of Processing

<!-- participants: GDPR, NIS2, CRA, DORA, AI_Act -->

##### Participants

| Regulation | SO ID | 1-line summary | Scope/angle |
|---|---|---|---|
| GDPR | SO-GDPR-029 + SO-GDPR-038 | Records of processing activities per Art. 30(1)/(2); transparency to data subjects on processing operations (Art. 12/13/14). | Controller/processor RoPA mandate; 250-employee exception; data-subject-rights-supporting records. |
| NIS2 | SO-NIS2-024 (partial) | Use of certified ICT products/services/processes where required by Member State or Commission delegated act (Art. 24). | Certification-driven recordkeeping, not a RoPA-equivalent. |
| CRA | SO-CRA-055/056/057/058/059/061/062/063/064/065/066/067 (many) | Single point of contact + product identification + 10-year technical documentation retention + EU declaration + CE marking + Annex VII content + corrective measures + MSA cooperation + cessation of operations. | Product-level technical documentation regime; Annex VII 8-item content list; 10-year retention or support period (whichever longer). |
| DORA | (out-of-scope) | DORA does not impose a RoPA obligation (no GDPR Art. 30 analogue). | BC/DR logging obligations (Art. 12) are operational continuity artefacts, not RoPA-equivalents. |
| AI_Act | SO-AIACT-009 + SO-AIACT-010 (partial) | Technical documentation (Annex IV via Art. 11/12) + QMS documentation of 13 enumerated aspects. | AI-system-specific documentation mandate; partial coverage. GDPR Art. 30 is the dedicated authority for RoPA. |

##### Pairwise matrix

<!-- pair: GDPR,NIS2 -->
| GDPR ↔ NIS2 | **different-perspective** | Tension/difference |
|---|---|---|
| **GDPR** (SR-GDPR-048, Art. 30(1)-(4); SR-GDPR-050, Art. 30(3) integrity; SR-GDPR-065-068, Art. 12-14 transparency) | RoPA + supervisory-authority access + transparent privacy notices + data-subject rights. | |
|   → full rationale: `../../../Regulation/GDPR/02_SecurityRules_NIST.md` SR-GDPR-048 / SR-GDPR-050 / SR-GDPR-065-068 |
| **NIS2** (SR-NIS2-043, Art. 24(1)/(2)/(3) — partial) | Use of certified ICT products/services/processes. | |
|   → full rationale: `../../../Regulation/NIS2/02_SecurityRules_NIST.md` SR-NIS2-043 |

**Why different-perspective**: GDPR RoPA is processing-activities-focussed; NIS 2 Art. 24 is certification-driven. Different record types; no substantive overlap.

|   → full rationale: `../../../Regulation/GDPR/02_SecurityRules_NIST.md` SR-GDPR-048 |
| **CRA** (SR-CRA-085, Art. 13(12) pre-market docs + EU declaration + CE; SR-CRA-087, Annex VII §1-§2 general description + design/development; SR-CRA-088, Annex VII §5-§8 standards + tests + declaration + SBOM) | Technical documentation + EU declaration + CE marking + Annex VII 8-item content. | |
|   → full rationale: `../../../Regulation/CRA/02_SecurityRules_NIST.md` SR-CRA-085 / SR-CRA-087 / SR-CRA-088 |

**Why different-perspective**: GDPR RoPA covers processing activities (purposes, data categories, recipients, safeguards); CRA technical documentation covers product cybersecurity (design, development, risk assessment, SBOM, conformity). Different artefacts serving different audit purposes. The two regimes do not cross-substitute.

|   → full rationale: `../../../Regulation/AI_Act/02_SecurityRules_NIST.md` SR-AIACT-009 / SR-AIACT-010 / SR-AIACT-017 |

|   → full rationale: `../../../Regulation/NIS2/02_SecurityRules_NIST.md` SR-NIS2-043 |
| **CRA** (SR-CRA-078, Art. 27(8)/(9) CSA-certification presumption) | Presumption of conformity via CSA certification. | |
|   → full rationale: `../../../Regulation/CRA/02_SecurityRules_NIST.md` SR-CRA-078 |

**Why complementary**: NIS 2 Art. 24 requires use of certified products; CRA Art. 27(8)/(9) provides the CSA-certification presumption-of-conformity machinery. The two regimes link at the certification boundary.


<!-- /pair -->

|---|---|---|

**Why complementary**: CRA Annex VII + AI Act Annex IV + AI Act Art. 17 QMS stack for AI-enabled products. No OJ-level consolidation rule for documentation (unlike the CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 risk-assessment consolidation).


<!-- /pair -->

##### Emergent tensions (4 regulations)
<!-- emergent: GDPR,NIS2,CRA,AI_Act -->

- **Four documentation regimes with overlapping but non-substitutable artefacts**: GDPR RoPA, NIS 2 certification-driven records (Art. 24), CRA Annex VII 8-item technical documentation + 10-year retention, AI Act Annex IV + Art. 17 QMS. All four apply to a DORA-excluded entity that processes personal data and ships AI-enabled CRA-regulated products and is an NSIE. No OJ-level consolidation rule exists (the CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 consolidation applies only to risk assessment, not documentation). Phase 1 entities must maintain four parallel documentation regimes.
- **DORA is fully absent**: DORA does not impose a RoPA-equivalent. DORA BC/DR logging (Art. 12) is operational continuity, not processing-of-personal-data records. For DORA financial entities, the GDPR RoPA remains the dedicated authority for processing-activities documentation. The DORA Art. 12 logging obligation is a separate artefact.

<!-- /emergent -->

##### SR cross-validation
The 4 applicable regulations map D-09.4 to the NIST CSF 2.0 Subcategories **ID.AM-08** (Systems, hardware, software, services, and data are managed throughout their life cycle) and **PR.DS-12** (Data managed consistent with risk strategy) as the convergent pair. GDPR additionally maps **GV.PO-02** (Cybersecurity processes and procedures established, communicated, enforced) for the demonstrable-compliance dimension; CRA additionally maps **ID.RA-05** for the risk-assessment documentation.

##### Downstream implication
Phase 1 entities must implement four parallel documentation regimes. The retention periods differ (GDPR until processing ceases + supervisory-authority request; CRA 10 years or support period, whichever longer; AI Act Art. 19(1) 6-month log retention; NIS 2 Art. 24 per Member State). The Layer 1 cross-cutting analysis is required to reconcile retention cadences.
